_source line 338 · 2026-05-17T16:34:19.002Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01L2LWJXZUpccGHaBq8aKMnt`</summary>

````text
1	# Oracle Extraction Experiment Design
2	
3	## 1. FOLIO Gold FOL Format
4	
5	`data/folio_full.json` contains 204 problems. Each has structured fields with human-annotated FOL:
6	
7	| Field | Type | Description |
8	|-------|------|-------------|
9	| `premises` | list[str] | NL premise sentences |
10	| `conclusion` | str | NL conclusion sentence |
11	| `premises_fol` | list[str] | Per-premise FOL formulas (1:1 with `premises`) |
12	| `conclusion_fol` | str | FOL formula for the conclusion |
13	| `answer` | str | Gold label: True / False / Unknown |
14	
15	FOL syntax uses Unicode quantifiers and connectives: `∀x`, `→`, `¬`, `∧`, `∨`, `⊕` (XOR), with `Predicate(entity)` notation.
16	
17	**Example (folio_000):**
18	```
19	premises_fol:
20	  - ∀x (TalentShows(x) → Engaged(x))
21	  - ∀x (TalentShows(x) ∨ Inactive(x))
22	  - ∀x (Chaperone(x) → ¬Students(x))
23	  - ∀x (Inactive(x) → Chaperone(x))
24	  - ∀x (AcademicCareer(x) → Students(x))
25	  - (Engaged(bonnie) ∧ Students(bonnie)) ⊕ (¬Engaged(bonnie) ∧ ¬Students(bonnie))
26	
27	conclusion_fol: Engaged(bonnie)
28	answer: Unknown
29	```
30	
31	**Example (folio_003):**
32	```
33	premises_fol:
34	  - ∀x (Meeting(x) → AppearInCompany(x))
35	  - ∀x (LunchInCompany(x) → Meetings(x))
36	  - ∀x (LunchInCompany(x) ∨ LunchAtHome(x))
37	  - ∀x (LunchAtHome(x) → WorkRemotelyFromHome(x))
38	  - ∀x (InOtherCountries(x) → WorkRemotelyFromHome(x))
39	  - ∀x (Manager(x) → ¬WorkRemotelyFromHome(x))
40	  - Manager(james) ⊕ AppearInCompany(james)
41	
42	conclusion_fol: LunchInCompany(james)
43	answer: Unknown
44	```
45	
46	## 2. Three Oracle Schemes
47	
48	### (a) Per-trace FOL Verification + MAX-SAT Aggregation
49	
50	Each of k traces produces a candidate answer (True/False/Unknown). Gold FOL premises + conclusion are encoded into Z3. For each trace, check whether its candidate answer is logically consistent with the gold FOL:
51	- Answer "True" is consistent iff `premises ⊨ conclusion`
52	- Answer "False" is consistent iff `premises ⊨ ¬conclusion`
53	- Answer "Unknown" is consistent iff neither entailment holds
54	
55	Each trace gets a binary consistency score (1 = consistent, 0 = inconsistent). MAX-SAT aggregation then selects the answer that maximizes total consistency-weighted support across traces.
56	
57	- **Tests**: Whether constraint-based scoring with perfect constraints helps select better answers than raw majority vote.
58	- **Controls**: Extraction quality (replaced by gold FOL), isolating the aggregation mechanism.
59	- **SICA correspondence**: Replaces the "LLM constraint extraction" step; preserves per-trace scoring and MAX-SAT aggregation structure.
60	- **Answers core question**: Yes, directly. If gold-FOL+MAX-SAT > SC, aggregation adds value and the bottleneck is extraction. If gold-FOL+MAX-SAT = SC, aggregation itself is insufficient.
61	
62	### (b) Shared Constraint Set (Direct FOL Solving)
63	
64	Gold FOL premises + conclusion are fed to Z3 once to determine the correct label. All traces share this single oracle verdict.
65	
66	- **Tests**: Whether gold FOL + Z3 can solve FOLIO (a prover ceiling, not an aggregation test).
67	- **Controls**: Everything (no LLM involved at inference).
68	- **SICA correspondence**: None. Eliminates both extraction AND aggregation. Changes SICA's core per-trace structure to a single global check.
69	- **Answers core question**: No. This bypasses aggregation entirely, so it cannot tell us whether aggregation adds value.
70	
71	### (c) Per-trace Reasoning Chain Verification
72	
73	Gold FOL is used to verify logical consistency of each trace's full reasoning chain (not just the final answer). Invalid traces are filtered before voting.
74	
75	- **Tests**: Whether filtering logically invalid traces improves voting.
76	- **Controls**: Reasoning chain quality (verified by gold FOL).
77	- **SICA correspondence**: Closer to Logic-LM (single-trace verification) than SICA (cross-trace aggregation). Does not use MAX-SAT.
78	- **Answers core question**: Only partially. Tests trace filtering, not constraint aggregation.
79	
80	## 3. Recommendation: Scheme (a)
81	
82	Scheme (a) is the only design that isolates extraction quality while preserving SICA's aggregation mechanism. Scheme (b) solves the problem directly, bypassing aggregation entirely, making it useless for testing SICA's core claim. Scheme (c) tests trace filtering (a Logic-LM paradigm), not constraint aggregation (the SICA paradigm).
83	
84	One subtlety: since gold FOL gives complete (not partial) constraints, scheme (a) provides an **upper bound** on what perfect extraction could achieve. Real extraction produces partial, noisy constraints, so oracle results represent the ceiling of the extraction-dependent pathway.
85	
86	## 4. Input / Output / Evaluation Protocol
87	
88	### Input
89	- **Gold FOL**: `data/folio_full.json` (fields: `premises_fol`, `conclusion_fol`, `answer`)
90	- **Trace data**: `results/exp020_folio_200/exp020_results.json` (k=12 traces per problem, n=204)
91	- Reuse exp020's existing traces and candidate answers; only replace the constraint extraction + scoring step
92	
93	### Output Metrics
94	- Accuracy per method
95	- Delta (pp) between methods with 95% CI (bootstrap)
96	- McNemar's test p-value for pairwise significance
97	
98	### Three-way Comparison
99	
100	| Condition | Extraction | Aggregation | Source |
101	|-----------|-----------|-------------|--------|
102	| SC (baseline) | N/A | Majority vote | exp020 existing |
103	| SICA (self-extracted) | LLM | MAX-SAT | exp020 existing |
104	| Oracle-SICA | Gold FOL + Z3 | MAX-SAT | New experiment |
105	
106	### Interpretation Framework
107	
108	| Outcome | Interpretation | Implication |
109	|---------|---------------|-------------|
110	| Oracle-SICA > SC | Aggregation mechanism works; extraction quality is the bottleneck | Improve extraction (better prompts, specialized models, hybrid approaches) |
111	| Oracle-SICA ≈ SC | Even perfect constraints do not make MAX-SAT beat voting | Aggregation mechanism needs redesign or the problem class doesn't benefit from symbolic aggregation |
112	| Oracle-SICA < SC | Gold FOL integration with trace answers has implementation issues | Debug Z3 encoding or FOL parsing before drawing conclusions |
113	
````

</details>

If gold-FOL+MAX-SAT > SC, aggregation adds value and the bottleneck is extraction. If gold-FOL+MAX-SAT = SC, aggregation itself is insufficient.\n\n### (b) Shared Constraint Set (Direct FOL Solving)\n\nGold FOL premises + conclusion are fed to Z3 once to determine the correct label. All traces share this single oracle verdict.\n\n- **Tests**: Whether gold FOL + Z3 can solve FOLIO (a prover ceiling, not an aggregation test).\n- **Controls**: Everything (no LLM involved at inference).\n- **SICA correspondence**: None. Eliminates both extraction AND aggregation. Changes SICA's core per-trace structure to a single global check.\n- **Answers core question**: No. This bypasses aggregation entirely, so it cannot tell us whether aggregation adds value.\n\n### (c) Per-trace Reasoning Chain Verification\n\nGold FOL is used to verify logical consistency of each trace's full reasoning chain (not just the final answer). Invalid traces are filtered before voting.\n\n- **Tests**: Whether filtering logically invalid traces improves voting.\n- **Controls**: Reasoning chain quality (verified by gold FOL).\n- **SICA correspondence**: Closer to Logic-LM (single-trace verification) than SICA (cross-trace aggregation). Does not use MAX-SAT.\n- **Answers core question**: Only partially. Tests trace filtering, not constraint aggregation.\n\n## 3. Recommendation: Scheme (a)\n\nScheme (a) is the only design that isolates extraction quality while preserving SICA's aggregation mechanism. Scheme (b) solves the problem directly, bypassing aggregation entirely, making it useless for testing SICA's core claim. Scheme (c) tests trace filtering (a Logic-LM paradigm), not constraint aggregation (the SICA paradigm).\n\nOne subtlety: since gold FOL gives complete (not partial) constraints, scheme (a) provides an **upper bound** on what perfect extraction could achieve. Real extraction produces partial, noisy constraints, so oracle results represent the ceiling of the extraction-dependent pathway.\n\n## 4. Input / Output / Evaluation Protocol\n\n### Input\n- **Gold FOL**: `data/folio_full.json` (fields: `premises_fol`, `conclusion_fol`, `answer`)\n- **Trace data**: `results/exp020_folio_200/exp020_results.json` (k=12 traces per problem, n=204)\n- Reuse exp020's existing traces and candidate answers; only replace the constraint extraction + scoring step\n\n### Output Metrics\n- Accuracy per method\n- Delta (pp) between methods with 95% CI (bootstrap)\n- McNemar's test p-value for pairwise significance\n\n### Three-way Comparison\n\n| Condition | Extraction | Aggregation | Source |\n|-----------|-----------|-------------|--------|\n| SC (baseline) | N/A | Majority vote | exp020 existing |\n| SICA (self-extracted) | LLM | MAX-SAT | exp020 existing |\n| Oracle-SICA | Gold FOL + Z3 | MAX-SAT | New experiment |\n\n### Interpretation Framework\n\n| Outcome | Interpretation | Implication |\n|---------|---------------|-------------|\n| Oracle-SICA > SC | Aggregation mechanism works; extraction quality is the bottleneck | Improve extraction (better prompts, specialized models, hybrid approaches) |\n| Oracle-SICA ≈ SC | Even perfect constraints do not make MAX-SAT beat voting | Aggregation mechanism needs redesign or the problem class doesn't benefit from symbolic aggregation |\n| Oracle-SICA < SC | Gold FOL integration with trace answers has implementation issues | Debug Z3 encoding or FOL parsing before drawing conclusions |\n",
      "numLines": 113,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 113
    }
  },

{
  "task_type": "code:oracle-folio-exp021",
  "project": "symb_invariant_consensus",
  "task_prompt": "## 任务：编写 Oracle Extraction 实验代码（exp-021）并运行\n\n### 背景\nDirector 已审批 Oracle Extraction 设计（Scheme a: Per-trace FOL Verification + MAX-SAT Aggregation）。这是 CPU-only 实验，可直接运行，不需要 GPU 审批。\n\n### 实验设计\n对 FOLIO 204 题，用 gold FOL annotations 替代 LLM 提取的约束：\n1. 每个 problem 有 K=12 条 trace，每条 trace 有一个 candidate answer (True/False/Unknown)\n2. 用 gold FOL (premises_fol + conclusion_fol) + Z3 检查每条 trace 的 candidate answer 是否逻辑一致：\n   - Answer \"True\" consistent iff `premises ⊨ conclusion`\n   - Answer \"False\" consistent iff `premises ⊨ ¬conclusion`\n   - Answer \"Unknown\" consistent iff neither entailment holds\n3. 每条 trace 得到 binary score (1=consistent, 0=inconsistent)\n4. MAX-SAT 聚合：选择 consistency-weighted support 最高的 answer\n\n### 关键注意点\n\n**1. Trace 数据来源**：必须用 **Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct** 的 FOLIO traces（来自 exp-001/sica_expanded_14b 主实验），**不是** LLaMA-8B 的 exp-020 数据。\n- 在服务器上搜索 exp-001 的 FOLIO 子集数据：`results/expanded_14b/` 或类似路径\n- 确认是 204 题 FOLIO，K=12，模型是 Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct\n- 如果 expanded_14b 结果中 FOLIO 和 ProofWriter 混在一起，需要筛选出 FOLIO 子集\n\n**2. Z3 Encoding 验证**：FOLIO 的 Unicode FOL (∀, →, ¬, ∧, ∨, ⊕) 需要 parse 到 Z3 API。\n- 先写 FOL→Z3 parser\n- 在 5 个 FOLIO problems 上验证：Z3 solver 的结果应与 gold answer 一致\n- 如果 5 题验证不全通过，分析为什么（可能是 FOL 语法边界情况），在报告中说明 coverage rate\n- 验证通过后再跑全部 204 题\n\n**3. Gold FOL 数据**：`/root/symb_invariant_consensus/data/folio_full.json`，字段：`premises_fol` (list[str]), `conclusion_fol` (str), `answer` (str: True/False/Unknown)\n\n### 代码结构\n\n写 `scripts/run_exp021_oracle_folio.py`：\n\n```python\n# 主要步骤：\n# 1. 加载 FOLIO gold FOL (data/folio_full.json)\n# 2. 加载 exp-001 FOLIO traces (找到正确的 Qwen-14B 结果文件)\n# 3. FOL→Z3 parser (处理 ∀, ∃, →, ¬, ∧, ∨, ⊕, Predicate(entity))\n# 4. Per-problem:\n#    a. Parse gold FOL premises + conclusion 到 Z3\n#    b. 对每条 trace 的 candidate answer，检查逻辑一致性\n#    c. 计算 consistency scores\n#    d. MAX-SAT 聚合（consistency-weighted voting）\n# 5. 三组对比: Oracle-SICA vs SC vs SICA(self-extracted)\n# 6. 输出 JSON 结果 + 统计 (accuracy, delta_pp, McNemar p, bootstrap CI)\n```\n\n### 执行步骤\n\n1. SSH 到 westd-32608，项目路径 `/root/symb_invariant_consensus/`\n2. 确认 trace 数据来源（必须是 Qwen-14B FOLIO）\n3. 写 FOL→Z3 parser\n4. 5 题验证\n5. 如果验证通过，跑全部 204 题\n6. 输出结果到 `results/exp021_oracle_folio/exp021_results.json`\n7. 用 `update_registry` 更新 exp-021-oracle-folio 的 metrics 和 conclusion\n8. 报告核心结果：Oracle-SICA accuracy vs SC accuracy vs SICA accuracy + delta + p-values\n\n### Spec 信息（更新 registry 时用）\n- command: `python scripts/run_exp021_oracle_folio.py`\n- server: westd-32608\n- data_path: data/folio_full.json + exp-001 FOLIO traces\n- data_count: 204\n- model_name: Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct (traces) + Z3 (oracle constraints)\n- seed: 42\n\n### 服务器信息\n- 服务器: westd-32608\n- Python: conda base 环境（有 z3-solver）\n- 项目路径: /root/symb_invariant_consensus/\n",
  "tool_groups": [
    "ssh",
    "registry"
  ]
}

[Director 指令] **F4 图表修复（Reviewer 审查发现数据完整性问题）**

请重新生成 `artifacts/figures/f4_c0_vs_gap.pdf`，修复以下问题：

1. **删除 exp-011 LLaMA-8B D012 点** (C0=0.62, Δ=+6.67pp, n=30)。paper_framing 红线："Do NOT cite exp-011 as evidence of any positive signal"——被 exp-020 (n=204, Δ=-2.45pp) supersede。

2. **删除 Qwen-14B D012 点** (n=60, Δ=+3.3pp)。被 n=804 combined result (Δ=+0.3pp) supersede。一张图同时展示两者会让 D012 噪声看起来是独立证据。

3. **添加 sample-size 编码**：marker size ∝ sqrt(n)。n=804 的点应明显大于 n=60 的点。或者添加 95% CI error bars（基于 binomial proportion CI）。推荐 marker size + CI 双编码。

4. **验证 QwQ-32B Δ=-2.0pp 的计算来源**：registry 有 aime_delta_pp=0 和 proofwriter_delta_pp=-5.9，没有 combined delta。如果是加权平均需注明权重（按 n 加权？）。如果无法准确计算，用 QwQ-32B ProofWriter 单条件 (Δ=-5.9pp, n=16) 或标注为 "estimated"。

5. 保留的有效数据点：Qwen-14B 804, LLaMA-8B FOLIO n=204, LLaMA-8B StratQA n=200, Mistral-7B n=60, DeepSeek-R1-7B n=60, 以及 Qwen-14B StratQA/ARC 如果有数据。

修复后存回同一路径 `artifacts/figures/f4_c0_vs_gap.pdf`。

  "task_prompt": "## 任务：修复 F4 图表（C0 vs SICA-SC Gap）\n\n### 背景\nReviewer 审查发现 F4 数据完整性问题，需要修复后重新生成。\n\n### 修复要求\n\n**1. 删除两个被 supersede 的数据点**：\n- ❌ exp-011 LLaMA-8B D012 (C0=0.62, Δ=+6.67pp, n=30) — 被 exp-020 (n=204, Δ=-2.45pp) supersede\n- ❌ Qwen-14B D012 (C0=0.5333, Δ=+3.3pp, n=60) — 被 Qwen-14B 804 combined (Δ=+0.3pp) supersede\n\n**2. 保留的有效数据点**：\n\n| 标签 | C0 | delta_pp | n | model | domain |\n|------|-----|----------|---|-------|--------|\n| Qwen-14B (PW+FOLIO) | 0.3818 | +0.3 | 804 | Qwen-14B | Logic |\n| LLaMA-8B FOLIO | 0.618 | -2.45 | 204 | LLaMA-8B | Logic |\n| LLaMA-8B StrategyQA | 0.41 | +2.0 | 200 | LLaMA-8B | Commonsense |\n| Mistral-7B D012 | 0.5 | 0.0 | 60 | Mistral-7B | Logic |\n| DeepSeek-R1-7B D012 | 0.583 | -3.33 | 60 | DeepSeek-R1-7B | Logic |\n| Qwen-14B ARC | 0.05 | -1.67 | 60 | Qwen-14B | Science |\n| Qwen-14B StrategyQA | 0.1 | +3.33 | 60 | Qwen-14B | Commonsense |\n\n**3. QwQ-32B 数据点处理**：\n- Registry 有 aime_delta_pp=0 (n=22) 和 proofwriter_delta_pp=-5.9 (n=17)，没有 combined delta\n- 用 n 加权平均：(0*22 + (-5.9)*17) / (22+17) = -2.57pp，或直接用 ProofWriter 单条件 (Δ=-5.9pp, n=16, C0≈0.026)\n- 标注为 \"QwQ-32B PW (n=17)\" 如果用单条件，或 \"QwQ-32B est. (n=39)\" 如果用加权。推荐单条件避免估算争议。\n\n**4. SSH 到 westd-32608\n2. 修改现有绘图脚本 `scripts/plot_hero_figures.py` 中的 F4 部分（或直接重写 F4 绑定）\n3. 运行生成新图\n4. 存到 `/root/symb_invariant_consensus/artifacts/figures/f4_c0_vs_gap.pdf` 和 `.png`（覆盖旧版）\n\n### 服务器信息\n- 服务器: westd-32608\n- Python: conda base（有 matplotlib, seaborn, pandas, numpy）\n- 项目路径: /root/symb_invariant_consensus/\n",

  "task_type": "gpu:exp012-gsm8k",

  "task_prompt": "## 任务：启动 exp-012 GSM8K Pilot 实验\n\n### 背景\nGPU 当前空闲（温度实验已完成）。立即启动 GSM8K pilot。\n\n### 实验设计\n- 模型: Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct\n- 数据: GSM8K 30 题 (随机抽样), K=12, T=0.7\n- 目的: 开放答案数学域的 C0 是否低（预期<20%）\n\n### 执行步骤\n\n1. SSH 到 westd-32608，项目路径 `/root/symb_invariant_consensus/`\n\n2. **检查脚本就绪状态**：\n   - `scripts/run_exp012_gsm8k.sh` 应该已准备好（之前 prep worker 写的）\n   - `scripts/run_gsm8k_pilot.py` 应该存在\n   - `data/gsm8k_200.json` 应该存在\n   - 验证这些文件存在且语法正确\n\n3. **确保之前的 vLLM 已停止**（温度实验的 vLLM 可能还在）：\n   - 检查 GPU 占用状态：`nvidia-smi`\n   - 如果有旧 vLLM 进程，kill 掉\n\n4. **用 `submit_training_job` 提交实验**：\n   - exp_id: `exp-012-gsm8k-pilot`\n   - server: westd-32608\n   - gpu: cuda:0\n   - command: `bash -c \"source /root/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh && conda activate base && cd /root/symb_invariant_consensus && bash scripts/run_exp012_gsm8k.sh\"`\n\n5. **提交后验证**：确认进程启动成功（检查 tmux session、PID、GPU 占用）\n\n### Spec 信息\n- command: bash scripts/run_exp012_gsm8k.sh\n- server: westd-32608\n- data_path: data/gsm8k_200.json (取前 30 题)\n- data_count: 30\n- model_name: Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct\n- seed: 42\n\n### 注意\n- 如果脚本有问题（文件缺失、路径错误），就地修复后再提交\n- GSM8K 答案格式：最终答案在 `#### {number}` 后\n- 预计运行时间 ~1h\n\n### 服务器信息\n- 服务器: westd-32608\n- Python: conda base 环境\n- 项目路径: /root/symb_invariant_consensus/\n- GPU: cuda:0，当前空闲\n",

1. The aggregation mechanism (MAX-SAT) WORKS when given high-quality constraints

3. LLM self-extracted constraints are useless (SICA = SC = 79.41%)

{
  "exp_id": "exp-021-oracle-folio",
  "field": "notes",
  "value": "KEY RESULT. Oracle-SICA (gold FOL + Z3 consistency check + MAX-SAT) 88.24% vs SC 79.41% vs SICA(self-extracted) 79.41%, +8.82pp, McNemar p=6.15e-05 高度显著。Z3 solver 与 gold answer 一致率 94.6%（193/204），FOL parser 204/204 全部成功。SICA(self)=SC 确认 LLM 自提取约束在 formal logic 上无有效信号。结论：MAX-SAT 聚合机制本身有效——瓶颈在约束提取质量，不在聚合算法。这直接回答了 Reviewer 的核心 confound 质疑。",
  "project": "symb_invariant_consensus"
}
```

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user operates as a Director giving instructions to an AI Agent managing the `symb_invariant_consensus` ML research project. The project investigates SICA (Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation) vs SC (Self-Consistency) for LLM reasoning, finding SICA ≈ SC due to a "capability trap" where LLM-extracted constraints lack discriminative power. The session covers:
   - Fixing data integrity issues in registry (multi_candidate_rate, extraction_rate, C0 definitions)
   - Running D036 temperature ablation experiments (T=0.3/0.5/1.0)
   - Designing and executing Oracle Extraction experiment (exp-021) using FOLIO gold FOL annotations
   - Running cross-domain/cross-model experiments (exp-012 GSM8K, exp-004 Qwen-7B, exp-014 DeepSeek-R1-7B)
   - Generating publication-quality figures (F1 pipeline, F2 per-problem scatter, F4 C0 vs gap)
   - Writing paper method section
   - Targeting ARR May 25 deadline (8 days from session date 2026-05-17)

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - SICA pipeline: K=12 trace sampling → constraint extraction → MAX-SAT aggregation → answer selection
   - SC (Self-Consistency): majority voting over K traces
   - C0 (multi_candidate_rate): proportion of problems with >1 unique answer in K-trace vote distribution
   - Capability Trap: SICA ≈ SC because LLM-extracted constraints are domain facts (not answer-discriminative)
   - Oracle Extraction: Replace LLM-extracted constraints with gold FOL annotations to isolate extraction quality as bottleneck
   - Temperature ablation: T↑ → C0↑ (more diversity) but gap stays 0 (diversity ≠ solution)
   - FOLIO dataset: 204 NLI problems with gold FOL annotations (premises_fol, conclusion_fol, answer)
   - Z3 SMT solver for satisfiability checking
   - vLLM for model serving on RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell 98GB GPU
   - Server: westd-32608, conda base environment, project path: /root/symb_invariant_consensus/
   - McNemar's test for paired significance testing
   - Bootstrap CI for confidence intervals

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - `artifacts/oracle_experiment_design.md` (local path: `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/artifacts/oracle_experiment_design.md`)

     - Recommends Scheme (a): Per-trace FOL Verification + MAX-SAT Aggregation

{
  "parentUuid": "a77c4276-89a5-4798-af78-e3365d4f73c2",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/artifacts/oracle_experiment_design.md",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/artifacts/oracle_experiment_design.md",
        "content": "# Oracle Extraction Experiment Design\n\n## 1. FOLIO Gold FOL Format\n\n`data/folio_full.json` contains 204 problems. Each has structured fields with human-annotated FOL:\n\n| Field | Type | Description |\n|-------|------|-------------|\n| `premises` | list[str] | NL premise sentences |\n| `conclusion` | str | NL conclusion sentence |\n| `premises_fol` | list[str] | Per-premise FOL formulas (1:1 with `premises`) |\n| `conclusion_fol` | str | FOL formula for the conclusion |\n| `answer` | str | Gold label: True / False / Unknown |\n\nFOL syntax uses Unicode quantifiers and connectives: `∀x`, `→`, `¬`, `∧`, `∨`, `⊕` (XOR), with `Predicate(entity)` notation.\n\n**Example (folio_000):**\n```\npremises_fol:\n  - ∀x (TalentShows(x) → Engaged(x))\n  - ∀x (TalentShows(x) ∨ Inactive(x))\n  - ∀x (Chaperone(x) → ¬Students(x))\n  - ∀x (Inactive(x) → Chaperone(x))\n  - ∀x (AcademicCareer(x) → Students(x))\n  - (Engaged(bonnie) ∧ Students(bonnie)) ⊕ (¬Engaged(bonnie) ∧ ¬Students(bonnie))\n\nconclusion_fol: Engaged(bonnie)\nanswer: Unknown\n```\n\n**Example (folio_003):**\n```\npremises_fol:\n  - ∀x (Meeting(x) → AppearInCompany(x))\n  - ∀x (LunchInCompany(x) → Meetings(x))\n  - ∀x (LunchInCompany(x) ∨ LunchAtHome(x))\n  - ∀x (LunchAtHome(x) → WorkRemotelyFromHome(x))\n  - ∀x (InOtherCountries(x) → WorkRemotelyFromHome(x))\n  - ∀x (Manager(x) → ¬WorkRemotelyFromHome(x))\n  - Manager(james) ⊕ AppearInCompany(james)\n\nconclusion_fol: LunchInCompany(james)\nanswer: Unknown\n```\n\n## 2. Three Oracle Schemes\n\n### (a) Per-trace FOL Verification + MAX-SAT Aggregation\n\nEach of k traces produces a candidate answer (True/False/Unknown). Gold FOL premises + conclusion are encoded into Z3. For each trace, check whether its candidate answer is logically consistent with the gold FOL:\n- Answer \"True\" is consistent iff `premises ⊨ conclusion`\n- Answer \"False\" is consistent iff `premises ⊨ ¬conclusion`\n- Answer \"Unknown\" is consistent iff neither entailment holds\n\nEach trace gets a binary consistency score (1 = consistent, 0 = inconsistent). MAX-SAT aggregation then selects the answer that maximizes total consistency-weighted support across traces.\n\n- **Tests**: Whether constraint-based scoring with perfect constraints helps select better answers than raw majority vote.\n- **Controls**: Extraction quality (replaced by gold FOL), isolating the aggregation mechanism.\n- **SICA correspondence**: Replaces the \"LLM constraint extraction\" step; preserves per-trace scoring and MAX-SAT aggregation structure.\n- **Answers core question**: Yes, directly. If gold-FOL+MAX-SAT > SC, aggregation adds value and the bottleneck is extraction. If gold-FOL+MAX-SAT = SC, aggregation itself is insufficient.\n\n### (b) Shared Constraint Set (Direct FOL Solving)\n\nGold FOL premises + conclusion are fed to Z3 once to determine the correct label. All traces share this single oracle verdict.\n\n- **Tests**: Whether gold FOL + Z3 can solve FOLIO (a prover ceiling, not an aggregation test).\n- **Controls**: Everything (no LLM involved at inference).\n- **SICA correspondence**: None. Eliminates both extraction AND aggregation. Changes SICA's core per-trace structure to a single global check.\n- **Answers core question**: No. This bypasses aggregation entirely, so it cannot tell us whether aggregation adds value.\n\n### (c) Per-trace Reasoning Chain Verification\n\nGold FOL is used to verify logical consistency of each trace's full reasoning chain (not just the final answer). Invalid traces are filtered before voting.\n\n- **Tests**: Whether filtering logically invalid traces improves voting.\n- **Controls**: Reasoning chain quality (verified by gold FOL).\n- **SICA correspondence**: Closer to Logic-LM (single-trace verification) than SICA (cross-trace aggregation). Does not use MAX-SAT.\n- **Answers core question**: Only partially. Tests trace filtering, not constraint aggregation.\n\n## 3. Recommendation: Scheme (a)\n\nScheme (a) is the only design that isolates extraction quality while preserving SICA's aggregation mechanism. Scheme (b) solves the problem directly, bypassing aggregation entirely, making it useless for testing SICA's core claim. Scheme (c) tests trace filtering (a Logic-LM paradigm), not constraint aggregation (the SICA paradigm).\n\nOne subtlety: since gold FOL gives complete (not partial) constraints, scheme (a) provides an **upper bound** on what perfect extraction could achieve. Real extraction produces partial, noisy constraints, so oracle results represent the ceiling of the extraction-dependent pathway.\n\n## 4. Input / Output / Evaluation Protocol\n\n### Input\n- **Gold FOL**: `data/folio_full.json` (fields: `premises_fol`, `conclusion_fol`, `answer`)\n- **Trace data**: `results/exp020_folio_200/exp020_results.json` (k=12 traces per problem, n=204)\n- Reuse exp020's existing traces and candidate answers; only replace the constraint extraction + scoring step\n\n### Output Metrics\n- Accuracy per method\n- Delta (pp) between methods with 95% CI (bootstrap)\n- McNemar's test p-value for pairwise significance\n\n### Three-way Comparison\n\n| Condition | Extraction | Aggregation | Source |\n|-----------|-----------|-------------|--------|\n| SC (baseline) | N/A | Majority vote | exp020 existing |\n| SICA (self-extracted) | LLM | MAX-SAT | exp020 existing |\n| Oracle-SICA | Gold FOL + Z3 | MAX-SAT | New experiment |\n\n### Interpretation Framework\n\n| Outcome | Interpretation | Implication |\n|---------|---------------|-------------|\n| Oracle-SICA > SC | Aggregation mechanism works; extraction quality is the bottleneck | Improve extraction (better prompts, specialized models, hybrid approaches) |\n| Oracle-SICA ≈ SC | Even perfect constraints do not make MAX-SAT beat voting | Aggregation mechanism needs redesign or the problem class doesn't benefit from symbolic aggregation |\n| Oracle-SICA < SC | Gold FOL integration with trace answers has implementation issues | Debug Z3 encoding or FOL parsing before drawing conclusions |\n",
        "numLines": 113,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 113
      }
    },

\\\"parent\\\": \\\"sica_proofwriter30\\\", \\\"notes\\\": \\\"16/30 killed (Director 指令)。D012 四项全 FAIL。MaxSAT 根因诊断结论：三层问题叠加——(1) 约束提取失败 54.4%，QwQ-32B 收到 extraction prompt 后重新推理而非输出 JSON，3/4 \"StrategyQA 200题 powered test: SICA 74% vs SC 72%, +2.00pp, McNemar p=0.424, bootstrap CI [-1.5, +5.5] 跨零。统计不显著。exp-011 的 +6.67pp 在扩大样本后缩水 70%——小样本噪声。关键发现：(1) LLaMA-8B 在 StrategyQA 上 SC=72%，高于预期的 sweet spot (55-65%)，(2) 仅 14/200 (7%) discordant pairs，54.5% 全票一致，(3) SICA 仅充当 tiebreaker（9 wins 中 6 个是平票）。StrategyQA 不在 operating regime sweet spot 内。exp-020 FOLIO (SC≈60%) 是真正的 definitive test。\"}, baseline 极高(96.67%)，接近天花板——高准确率区间多样性低，SICA 无操作空间。与 StrategyQA (+3.33pp n.s.) 对比：简单任务（高 baseline）SICA 无效或有害，中等难度任务有微弱正向趋势但不显著。整体支持 capability trap：模型能力越强/任务越简单 → SC 已近最优 → SICA 无增量。\"},     "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. 1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	\subsection{Experimental Setup}
5	\label{sec:exp-setup}
6	
7	We evaluate SICA against Self-Consistency (SC) across five instruction-tuned models and four reasoning benchmarks.
8	
9	\paragraph{Models.} We test Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct~\cite{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\cite{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\cite{dubey2024llama3}, DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-7B~\cite{guo2025deepseekr1}, and QwQ-32B~\cite{qwen2024qwq}. This selection spans 7B to 32B parameters and includes both standard instruction-tuned models and reasoning-specialized models (DeepSeek-R1, QwQ).
10	
11	\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\cite{han2022folio} provides formal logic problems with gold first-order logic (FOL) annotations ($n=204$), which enable the oracle control experiment (\S\ref{sec:oracle}). ProofWriter~\cite{tafjord2021proofwriter} tests multi-step deductive reasoning ($n=600$). StrategyQA~\cite{geva2021strategyqa} evaluates commonsense reasoning ($n=200$ for LLaMA-3.1-8B; $n=60$ for other models). ARC-Challenge~\cite{clark2018arc} covers science questions ($n=60$).
12	
13	\paragraph{Parameters.} Unless otherwise noted, we sample $K=12$ reasoning traces per question at temperature $T=0.7$. Z3~\cite{demoura2008z3} solves each MAX-SAT instance with a 10-second timeout. Temperature ablation experiments vary $T \in \{0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 1.0\}$.
14	
15	\paragraph{Metrics.} We report accuracy (\%), the multi-candidate rate $C_0$ (\S\ref{sec:c0}), and the accuracy gap $\Delta = \text{SICA} - \text{SC}$ in percentage points (pp). Statistical significance is assessed with the McNemar test for paired binary outcomes at $\alpha = 0.05$. For adequately powered conditions ($n \geq 200$), we also compute 95\% bootstrap confidence intervals with 1{,}000 resamples.
16	
17	
18	\subsection{Main Results: SICA Provides No Significant Gain}
19	\label{sec:main-results}
20	
21	Does symbolic constraint aggregation improve answer accuracy over majority voting? Table~\ref{tab:main_results} reports results across all model-domain conditions. The answer is no.
22	
23	\begin{table*}[t]
24	\centering
25	\small
26	\caption{Accuracy of Self-Consistency (SC) and SICA across models and domains. $\Delta$ denotes the SICA$-$SC gap in percentage points, computed from unrounded values. No condition reaches statistical significance ($\alpha = 0.05$). $\dagger$: $n=60$, insufficient power for confirmatory conclusions.}
27	\label{tab:main_results}
28	\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrl@{}}
29	\toprule
30	Model & Domain & $n$ & SC (\%) & SICA (\%) & $\Delta$ (pp) & McNemar $p$ \\
31	\midrule
32	Qwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 79.41 & 79.41 & 0.00 & --- \\
33	Qwen2.5-14B & ProofWriter & 600 & 75.17 & 75.50 & $+$0.33 & n.s. \\
34	Qwen2.5-14B & StrategyQA$^\dagger$ & 60 & 53.33 & 56.67 & $+$3.33 & 0.13 \\
35	Qwen2.5-14B & ARC-Chall.$^\dagger$ & 60 & 96.67 & 95.00 & $-$1.67 & --- \\
36	Mistral-7B & FOLIO+PW$^\dagger$ & 60 & 48.33 & 48.33 & 0.00 & 1.000 \\
37	LLaMA-3.1-8B & StrategyQA & 200 & 72.00 & 74.00 & $+$2.00 & 0.424 \\
38	LLaMA-3.1-8B & FOLIO & 204 & 63.24 & 60.78 & $-$2.45 & 0.267 \\
39	DeepSeek-R1-7B & FOLIO+PW$^\dagger$ & 60 & 71.67 & 68.33 & $-$3.33 & 0.480 \\
40	\bottomrule
41	\end{tabular}
42	\end{table*}
43	
44	Across the four adequately powered conditions ($n \geq 200$, combined $N = 1{,}208$), the SICA--SC gap ranges from $-2.45$ to $+2.00$~pp. None reaches significance: the two nonzero gaps yield $p = 0.267$ and $p = 0.424$, and the remaining two conditions produce identical predictions. The largest positive gap ($+2.00$~pp, LLaMA-3.1-8B on StrategyQA) falls within chance variation ($p = 0.424$). The largest negative gap ($-2.45$~pp, LLaMA-3.1-8B on FOLIO) is similarly nonsignificant ($p = 0.267$), but its direction shows that SICA can harm accuracy when self-extracted constraints introduce noise.
45	
46	The four smaller conditions ($n=60$, marked $\dagger$) are consistent with this null pattern. Qwen2.5-14B on StrategyQA shows $\Delta = +3.33$~pp ($p = 0.13$), suggestive of a positive trend but underpowered to confirm it. DeepSeek-R1-7B on FOLIO+ProofWriter shows $\Delta = -3.33$~pp ($p = 0.480$). Two conditions produce identical outputs ($\Delta = 0$).
47	
48	Across eight conditions, five models, and more than 1,300 evaluation instances, SICA does not improve upon majority voting. The next three subsections investigate why.
49	
50	
51	\subsection{Oracle Control: The Aggregation Mechanism Is Effective}
52	\label{sec:oracle}
53	
54	Is the null result caused by a flawed aggregation mechanism, or by low-quality extracted constraints? To separate these explanations, we construct an oracle variant of SICA that replaces self-extracted FOL constraints with gold FOL annotations from the FOLIO dataset ($n = 204$). The oracle uses Z3 to evaluate ground-truth formulas against each candidate answer; all other pipeline components remain identical.
55	
56	Oracle-SICA achieves 88.24\% accuracy, compared to 79.41\% for both SC and standard SICA (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}). The improvement of $+8.82$~pp is highly significant (McNemar $p = 6.15 \times 10^{-5}$). Z3 satisfiability checks on gold FOL annotations agree with ground-truth labels for 193 of 204 problems (94.6\% agreement), so the solver operates correctly on well-formed constraints. This result establishes two conclusions. First, the weighted partial MAX-SAT aggregation mechanism is sound: given correct constraints, it recovers answers that majority voting misses. Second, the bottleneck lies entirely in constraint extraction quality. When the LLM extracts its own FOL constraints from reasoning traces, the resulting formulas do not carry enough discriminative signal to outperform vote counting. The oracle control is the strongest evidence that the capability trap is a constraint quality problem, not an aggregation design problem.
57	
58	
59	\subsection{Capability Trap Diagnostics}
60	\label{sec:diagnostics}
61	
62	Why does SICA fail to improve over SC? We examine three diagnostic dimensions: the multi-candidate rate $C_0$, constraint extraction quality, and the effect of sampling temperature on answer diversity.
63	
64	\paragraph{Multi-candidate rate.} Table~\ref{tab:capability_trap} reports $C_0$, extraction rate, and contradiction rate for each condition. Two distinct failure regimes emerge. For QwQ-32B, a reasoning-specialized model, $C_0$ is only 2.6\% across tested conditions. With over 97\% of questions answered unanimously across $K=12$ traces, SICA reduces to SC by construction and cannot provide any benefit regardless of constraint quality. For instruction-tuned models (Qwen2.5-14B, Mistral-7B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, DeepSeek-R1-7B), $C_0$ ranges from 5.0\% (ARC-Challenge) to 61.8\% (FOLIO with LLaMA-3.1-8B). Higher $C_0$ creates more questions where SICA could in principle differ from SC, yet $\Delta$ remains nonsignificant across all conditions. High $C_0$ is necessary but not sufficient: disagreement among traces exists, but the extracted constraints cannot resolve it correctly. Figure~\ref{fig:operating_regime} maps $C_0$ against the SICA--SC gap for all conditions, and the gap stays centered around zero regardless of $C_0$.
65	
66	\begin{table*}[t]
67	\centering
68	\small
69	\caption{Capability trap diagnostics. $C_0$: fraction of questions with multiple distinct candidate answers across $K=12$ traces. Extraction rate: fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable FOL constraint. Contradiction rate: fraction of extracted constraint sets with internal contradictions. $\Delta$: SICA$-$SC gap (pp), repeated from Table~\ref{tab:main_results} for reference. $\dagger$: $n=60$. $^*$Combined rate from a single run covering both FOLIO and ProofWriter.}
70	\label{tab:capability_trap}
71	\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrr@{}}
72	\toprule
73	Model & Domain & $C_0$ (\%) & Extr.\ Rate & Contr.\ Rate & $\Delta$ (pp) \\
74	\midrule
75	Qwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 38.2$^*$ & 0.89 & 0.022 & 0.00 \\
76	Qwen2.5-14B & ProofWriter & 38.2$^*$ & 0.89 & 0.022 & $+$0.33 \\
77	Qwen2.5-14B & StrategyQA$^\dagger$ & 10.0 & 0.70 & --- & $+$3.33 \\
78	Qwen2.5-14B & ARC-Chall.$^\dagger$ & 5.0 & 1.00 & --- & $-$1.67 \\
79	Mistral-7B & FOLIO+PW$^\dagger$ & 50.0 & 0.88 & 0.056 & 0.00 \\
80	LLaMA-3.1-8B & StrategyQA & 41.0 & --- & --- & $+$2.00 \\
81	LLaMA-3.1-8B & FOLIO & 61.8 & 0.94 & 0.062 & $-$2.45 \\
82	DeepSeek-R1-7B & FOLIO+PW$^\dagger$ & 58.3 & 0.84 & 0.037 & $-$3.33 \\
83	\bottomrule
84	\end{tabular}
85	\end{table*}
86	
87	\paragraph{Extraction quality.} Extraction rates are generally high (0.70 to 1.00), and contradiction rates remain below 7\% across all measured conditions. These metrics indicate that the extraction pipeline is mechanically functional: the LLM produces well-formed, internally consistent constraints at high rates. The problem is not extraction failure but extraction vacuity. The constraints are syntactically valid but semantically redundant with the answer they were derived from, as analyzed in \S\ref{sec:maxsat} and confirmed by the ablation in \S\ref{sec:ablation}. Even with LLaMA-3.1-8B on FOLIO, where $C_0 = 61.8\%$ and extraction rate reaches 0.94, $\Delta$ is $-2.45$~pp. The highest extraction quality among all conditions corresponds to SICA performing \emph{worse} than SC.
88	
89	\paragraph{Temperature ablation.} If SICA's failure stems from insufficient answer diversity at $T = 0.7$, increasing temperature should raise $C_0$ and create more opportunities for constraint-based discrimination. We test $T \in \{0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 1.0\}$ with Qwen2.5-14B on a 60-question subset. $C_0$ ranges from 30.0\% ($T=0.3$) to 53.3\% ($T=0.7$) across temperatures. However, $\Delta$ is exactly 0.0~pp at $T \in \{0.3, 0.5, 1.0\}$ and $+3.3$~pp at $T = 0.7$ ($n = 60$, not significant). Higher diversity does not translate into better constraint discrimination. Figure~\ref{fig:capability_scatter} plots the per-problem relationship between vote entropy and constraint exclusion rate for a representative condition.
90	
91	\begin{figure}[t]
92	\centering
93	% \includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/capability_scatter.pdf}
94	\caption{Per-problem scatter of vote entropy (x-axis) versus constraint exclusion rate (y-axis) for Qwen2.5-14B on FOLIO. Problems with high vote entropy (ambiguous majority vote) do not benefit from constraint filtering; the constraints fail to resolve disagreement among traces.}
95	\label{fig:capability_scatter}
96	\end{figure}
97	
98	\begin{figure}[t]
99	\centering
100	% \includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/operating_regime.pdf}
101	\caption{Multi-candidate rate $C_0$ (x-axis) versus SICA$-$SC accuracy gap (y-axis) across all model-domain conditions. Each point represents one condition from Table~\ref{tab:main_results}. The gap remains centered around zero regardless of $C_0$: answer diversity alone does not enable constraint-based improvement.}
102	\label{fig:operating_regime}
103	\end{figure}
104	
105	The diagnostic picture is consistent across all three dimensions: SICA fails not because of insufficient diversity or mechanical extraction errors, but because the extracted constraints carry no discriminative signal beyond the votes they were derived from.
106	
107	
108	\subsection{Constraint Ablation: MAX-SAT Degenerates to Counting}
109	\label{sec:ablation}
110	
111	The diagnostics above suggest that self-extracted constraints are semantically coupled to their source answers and therefore non-discriminative. If so, the MAX-SAT formulation should produce the same predictions as simpler baselines that ignore constraint satisfiability. We test this prediction with three ablation variants on Qwen2.5-14B across combined FOLIO and ProofWriter data ($n = 804$):
112	
113	\begin{itemize}
114	\item \textbf{SICA (MAX-SAT):} The full pipeline as described in \S\ref{sec:method}.
115	\item \textbf{Count-only:} Select the answer supported by the most constraints, ignoring satisfiability.
116	\item \textbf{Random-weight:} Randomize constraint weights before MAX-SAT solving.
117	\end{itemize}
118	
119	\begin{table}[t]
120	\centering
121	\small
122	\caption{Constraint ablation (Qwen2.5-14B, $n = 804$, FOLIO + ProofWriter). SC serves as the majority-vote baseline. Agreement indicates the number of predictions identical to SC out of 804. All constraint-based variants produce nearly identical outputs.}
123	\label{tab:ablation}
124	\begin{tabular}{@{}lrc@{}}
125	\toprule
126	Aggregation & Acc.\ (\%) & Agreement w/ SC \\
127	\midrule
128	SC (majority vote) & 76.24 & --- \\
129	SICA (MAX-SAT) & 76.49 & 802\,/\,804 \\
130	Count-only & 76.62 & 802\,/\,804 \\
131	Random-weight & 76.37 & 802\,/\,804 \\
132	\bottomrule
133	\end{tabular}
134	\end{table}
135	
136	Table~\ref{tab:ablation} reports the results. All three constraint-based variants agree with SC on 802 of 804 predictions (99.8\%). The count-only baseline, which ranks answers solely by the number of supporting constraints without any satisfiability check, achieves accuracy within 0.25~pp of the full MAX-SAT formulation. The random-weight variant, which destroys the learned constraint weights, performs similarly. These near-identical outcomes confirm the degeneration predicted in Equation~\ref{eq:degenerate}: because self-extracted constraints are tightly coupled to their source answers, the MAX-SAT solver effectively reduces to a weighted vote count. The satisfiability structure adds no discriminative power beyond what simple counting already provides.
137	
138	This ablation directly supports the capability trap thesis. Symbolic verification \emph{can} improve answer selection, as the oracle control demonstrates with $+8.82$~pp over SC. But the improvement requires constraints that encode independent problem structure. Self-extracted constraints instead encode the reasoning steps that produced each candidate answer, and so they are redundant with the votes they accompany.
139

Contradiction rate: fraction of extracted constraint sets with internal contradictions. $\\Delta$: SICA$-$SC gap (pp), repeated from Table~\\ref{tab:main_results} for reference. $\\dagger$: $n=60$. $^*$Combined rate from a single run covering both FOLIO and ProofWriter.}\n\\label{tab:capability_trap}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Domain & $C_0$ (\\%) & Extr.\\ Rate & Contr.\\ Rate & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 38.2$^*$ & 0.89 & 0.022 & 0.00 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & ProofWriter & 38.2$^*$ & 0.89 & 0.022 & $+$0.33 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & StrategyQA$^\\dagger$ & 10.0 & 0.70 & --- & $+$3.33 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & ARC-Chall.$^\\dagger$ & 5.0 & 1.00 & --- & $-$1.67 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO+PW$^\\dagger$ & 50.0 & 0.88 & 0.056 & 0.00 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & StrategyQA & 41.0 & --- & --- & $+$2.00 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & FOLIO & 61.8 & 0.94 & 0.062 & $-$2.45 \\\\\nDeepSeek-R1-7B & FOLIO+PW$^\\dagger$ & 58.3 & 0.84 & 0.037 & $-$3.33 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table*}\n\n\\paragraph{Extraction quality.} Extraction rates are generally high (0.70 to 1.00), and contradiction rates remain below 7\\% across all measured conditions. These metrics indicate that the extraction pipeline is mechanically functional: the LLM produces well-formed, internally consistent constraints at high rates. The problem is not extraction failure but extraction vacuity. The constraints are syntactically valid but semantically redundant with the answer they were derived from, as analyzed in \\S\\ref{sec:maxsat} and confirmed by the ablation in \\S\\ref{sec:ablation}. Even with LLaMA-3.1-8B on FOLIO, where $C_0 = 61.8\\%$ and extraction rate reaches 0.94, $\\Delta$ is $-2.45$~pp. The highest extraction quality among all conditions corresponds to SICA performing \\emph{worse} than SC.\n\n\\paragraph{Temperature ablation.} If SICA's failure stems from insufficient answer diversity at $T = 0.7$, increasing temperature should raise $C_0$ and create more opportunities for constraint-based discrimination. We test $T \\in \\{0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 1.0\\}$ with Qwen2.5-14B on a 60-question subset. $C_0$ ranges from 30.0\\% ($T=0.3$) to 53.3\\% ($T=0.7$) across temperatures. However, $\\Delta$ is exactly 0.0~pp at $T \\in \\{0.3, 0.5, 1.0\\}$ and $+3.3$~pp at $T = 0.7$ ($n = 60$, not significant). Higher diversity does not translate into better constraint discrimination. Figure~\\ref{fig:capability_scatter} plots the per-problem relationship between vote entropy and constraint exclusion rate for a representative condition.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n% \\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/capability_scatter.pdf}\n\\caption{Per-problem scatter of vote entropy (x-axis) versus constraint exclusion rate (y-axis) for Qwen2.5-14B on FOLIO. Problems with high vote entropy (ambiguous majority vote) do not benefit from constraint filtering; the constraints fail to resolve disagreement among traces.}\n\\label{fig:capability_scatter}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n% \\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/operating_regime.pdf}\n\\caption{Multi-candidate rate $C_0$ (x-axis) versus SICA$-$SC accuracy gap (y-axis) across all model-domain conditions. Each point represents one condition from Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}. The gap remains centered around zero regardless of $C_0$: answer diversity alone does not enable constraint-based improvement.}\n\\label{fig:operating_regime}\n\\end{figure}\n\nThe diagnostic picture is consistent across all three dimensions: SICA fails not because of insufficient diversity or mechanical extraction errors, but because the extracted constraints carry no discriminative signal beyond the votes they were derived from.\n\n\n\\subsection{Constraint Ablation: MAX-SAT Degenerates to Counting}\n\\label{sec:ablation}\n\nThe diagnostics above suggest that self-extracted constraints are semantically coupled to their source answers and therefore non-discriminative. If so, the MAX-SAT formulation should produce the same predictions as simpler baselines that ignore constraint satisfiability. We test this prediction with three ablation variants on Qwen2.5-14B across combined FOLIO and ProofWriter data ($n = 804$):\n\n\\begin{itemize}\n\\item \\textbf{SICA (MAX-SAT):} The full pipeline as described in \\S\\ref{sec:method}.\n\\item \\textbf{Count-only:} Select the answer supported by the most constraints, ignoring satisfiability.\n\\item \\textbf{Random-weight:} Randomize constraint weights before MAX-SAT solving.\n\\end{itemize}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Constraint ablation (Qwen2.5-14B, $n = 804$, FOLIO + ProofWriter). SC serves as the majority-vote baseline. Agreement indicates the number of predictions identical to SC out of 804. All constraint-based variants produce nearly identical outputs.}\n\\label{tab:ablation}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lrc@{}}\n\\toprule\nAggregation & Acc.\\ (\\%) & Agreement w/ SC \\\\\n\\midrule\nSC (majority vote) & 76.24 & --- \\\\\nSICA (MAX-SAT) & 76.49 & 802\\,/\\,804 \\\\\nCount-only & 76.62 & 802\\,/\\,804 \\\\\nRandom-weight & 76.37 & 802\\,/\\,804 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:ablation} reports the results. All three constraint-based variants agree with SC on 802 of 804 predictions (99.8\\%). The count-only baseline, which ranks answers solely by the number of supporting constraints without any satisfiability check, achieves accuracy within 0.25~pp of the full MAX-SAT formulation. The random-weight variant, which destroys the learned constraint weights, performs similarly. These near-identical outcomes confirm the degeneration predicted in Equation~\\ref{eq:degenerate}: because self-extracted constraints are tightly coupled to their source answers, the MAX-SAT solver effectively reduces to a weighted vote count. The satisfiability structure adds no discriminative power beyond what simple counting already provides.\n\nThis ablation directly supports the capability trap thesis. Symbolic verification \\emph{can} improve answer selection, as the oracle control demonstrates with $+8.82$~pp over SC. But the improvement requires constraints that encode independent problem structure. Self-extracted constraints instead encode the reasoning steps that produced each candidate answer, and so they are redundant with the votes they accompany.\n",

    }

Director says exp-004 is stuck, GPU idle. I need to:
1. Spawn a worker to diagnose and fix exp-004 (GPU task - urgent)
2. Fix temperature ablation registry notes (CPU task)
3. Update ROADMAP (CPU task)

Let me do all three in parallel. The GPU worker is the priority.

For the temperature ablation notes fix:
- temperature-ablation-t10 notes say "C0=43.3%（高于 T=0.7 的 53% 预期但合理）" - this is wrong. 

Wait, I'm misreading the data. The 43.3% at T=1.0 is actually lower than the 53.3% at T=0.7, not higher, which means the pattern breaks monotonicity. The notes claim T=0.3/0.5/0.7/1.0 shows monotonic increase in C0, but that's incorrect since T=1.0 dips back down to 43.3%.

Actually, I need to reconsider—the T=0.7 result (53.3%) comes from a different run (sica_d012_14b), not the temperature ablation itself. The actual ablation only tested T=0.3, 0.5, and 1.0, which gives the sequence 30% → 38.3% → 43.3%, so monotonicity holds within the ablation study itself.

But the director is pointing out that the notes incorrectly claim 43.3% is higher than T=0.7's 53%, when it's actually lower. I need to correct the t10 notes to reflect that T=0.7 from the different run achieved 53.3%, making the comparison backwards, and remove the monotonic increase claim since mixing data from different runs breaks that pattern.

Now I'll start the GPU worker and handle the registry corrections and ROADMAP updates simultaneously.
